    Model-checking game for the modal μ-calculus

    AbbreviationPosition of the μ-calculus model-checking game#✓fully proved

    Cslib.Modal.MuCalculus.Game.GamePosition

    A position of the μ-calculus model-checking game: the formula, the world, and the stack of enclosing fixpoint binders, innermost first.

    abbrev GamePosition (Atom World : Type u) :=
  MuFormula Atom × World × List (MuFormula Atom)
    inductive MuFormula (Atom : Type u) : Type u where
  | atom : Atom → MuFormula Atom
  | natom : Atom → MuFormula Atom
  | var : ℕ → MuFormula Atom
  | bot : MuFormula Atom
  | top : MuFormula Atom
  | and : MuFormula Atom → MuFormula Atom → MuFormula Atom
  | or : MuFormula Atom → MuFormula Atom → MuFormula Atom
  | box : MuFormula Atom → MuFormula Atom

    InductiveMoves of the μ-calculus model-checking game#✓fully proved

    Cslib.Modal.MuCalculus.Game.Move

    Fix a Kripke model \(M\) with accessibility relation \(R\). A game position \((\varphi, w, s)\) consists of a μ-calculus formula \(\varphi\) (with de Bruijn variables), a world \(w\) and a stack \(s\) of fixpoint binders, innermost first. The moves are exactly the following. From \((\varphi \wedge \psi, w, s)\) and from \((\varphi \vee \psi, w, s)\) to \((\varphi, w, s)\) and to \((\psi, w, s)\). From \((\Box \varphi, w, s)\) and from \((\Diamond \varphi, w, s)\) to \((\varphi, v, s)\) for every world \(v\) with \(w R v\). From \((\mu \varphi, w, s)\) to \((\varphi, w, s')\), where \(s'\) is \(s\) with \(\mu \varphi\) pushed on top, and likewise from \((\nu \varphi, w, s)\) to \((\varphi, w, s'')\), where \(s''\) is \(s\) with \(\nu \varphi\) pushed on top. From \((x_n, w, s)\), where \(x_n\) is the variable with de Bruijn index \(n\) and \(n\) is less than the length of \(s\), to \((s_n, w, t)\), where \(s_n\) is the entry of \(s\) at index \(n\) (counting from \(0\)) and \(t\) is \(s\) with its first \(n + 1\) entries removed.

    Cslib.Modal.MuCalculus.Game.Move · 29 lines 
    inductive Move (M : KripkeModel World Atom) :
    GamePosition Atom World → GamePosition Atom World → Prop
  /-- Refuter chooses a conjunct. -/
  | andLeft {φ ψ : MuFormula Atom} {w : World} {st : List (MuFormula Atom)} :
      Move M ⟨.and φ ψ, w, st⟩ ⟨φ, w, st⟩
  /-- Refuter chooses a conjunct. -/
  | andRight {φ ψ : MuFormula Atom} {w : World} {st : List (MuFormula Atom)} :
      Move M ⟨.and φ ψ, w, st⟩ ⟨ψ, w, st⟩
  /-- Verifier chooses a disjunct. -/
  | orLeft {φ ψ : MuFormula Atom} {w : World} {st : List (MuFormula Atom)} :
      Move M ⟨.or φ ψ, w, st⟩ ⟨φ, w, st⟩
  /-- Verifier chooses a disjunct. -/
  | orRight {φ ψ : MuFormula Atom} {w : World} {st : List (MuFormula Atom)} :
      Move M ⟨.or φ ψ, w, st⟩ ⟨ψ, w, st⟩
  /-- Refuter chooses a successor. -/
  | box {φ : MuFormula Atom} {w v : World} {st : List (MuFormula Atom)}
      (h : M.rel w v) : Move M ⟨.box φ, w, st⟩ ⟨φ, v, st⟩
  /-- Verifier chooses a successor. -/
  | dia {φ : MuFormula Atom} {w v : World} {st : List (MuFormula Atom)}
      (h : M.rel w v) : Move M ⟨.dia φ, w, st⟩ ⟨φ, v, st⟩
  /-- Unfold a least fixed point, pushing its binder on the stack. -/
  | mu {φ : MuFormula Atom} {w : World} {st : List (MuFormula Atom)} :
      Move M ⟨.mu φ, w, st⟩ ⟨φ, w, .mu φ :: st⟩
  /-- Unfold a greatest fixed point, pushing its binder on the stack. -/
  | nu {φ : MuFormula Atom} {w : World} {st : List (MuFormula Atom)} :
      Move M ⟨.nu φ, w, st⟩ ⟨φ, w, .nu φ :: st⟩
  /-- Jump back to the binder a variable refers to, restoring that binder's stack. -/
  | var {n : ℕ} {w : World} {st : List (MuFormula Atom)} (h : n < st.length) :
      Move M ⟨.var n, w, st⟩ ⟨st.getD n .bot, w, st.drop (n + 1)⟩
    structure KripkeModel (World : Type u) (Atom : Type v) where
  /-- There is at least one world. -/
  nonempty_world : Nonempty World
  /-- The accessibility relation. -/
  rel : World → World → Prop
  /-- The atoms true at each world. -/
  valuation : World → Atom → Prop

    DefinitionOwner of a game position#✓fully proved

    Cslib.Modal.MuCalculus.Game.Owner

    The owner of a game position \((\varphi, w, s)\) is Verifier when \(\varphi\) is a disjunction, a diamond formula or a greatest fixed point, and Refuter for every other formula.

    def Owner : GamePosition Atom World → Player
  | ⟨.or _ _, _, _⟩ | ⟨.dia _, _, _⟩ | ⟨.nu _, _, _⟩ => true
  | _ => false
    abbrev GamePosition (Atom World : Type u) :=
  MuFormula Atom × World × List (MuFormula Atom)

    DefinitionPriority of a game position#✓fully proved

    Cslib.Modal.MuCalculus.Game.prio

    Only fixpoint-binder positions carry a priority. For a formula \(\varphi\), a world \(w\) and a binder stack \(s\) of length \(k\), the position \((\mu X.\,\varphi, w, s)\), whose formula is the least fixpoint with body \(\varphi\), has priority \(2k+1\), the position \((\nu X.\,\varphi, w, s)\), whose formula is the greatest fixpoint with body \(\varphi\), has priority \(2k+2\), and every other position has no priority. Priorities strictly increase with nesting depth, so the outermost binder regenerated infinitely often has the least priority.

    This is the nesting-depth refinement of the alternation-depth priority assignment: same-type nesting counts toward the depth, which only refines the order (outer still strictly below inner) and keeps parity tied to the binder kind. Fixed priorities (\(\mu \mapsto 1\), \(\nu \mapsto 2\)) under the greatest-recurring-priority rule would let an inner vacuous \(\nu\) dominate an outer \(\mu\): in \(\mu X.\,\nu Y.\,X\), whose denotation is empty, Verifier would win the only play.

    def prio : GamePosition Atom World → Option ℕ
  | ⟨.mu _, _, st⟩ => some (2 * st.length + 1)
  | ⟨.nu _, _, st⟩ => some (2 * st.length + 2)
  | _ => none
    abbrev GamePosition (Atom World : Type u) :=
  MuFormula Atom × World × List (MuFormula Atom)

    DefinitionPositions with no moves#✓fully proved

    Cslib.Modal.MuCalculus.Game.NoMoves

    In a Kripke model \(M\), a game position \(p\) has no moves when there is no position \(q\) such that going from \(p\) to \(q\) is a move of the model-checking game in \(M\).

    def NoMoves (M : KripkeModel World Atom) (p : GamePosition Atom World) : Prop :=
  ∀ q, ¬ Move M p q
    structure KripkeModel (World : Type u) (Atom : Type v) where

    DefinitionWinner at a terminal position#✓fully proved

    Cslib.Modal.MuCalculus.Game.TerminalWin

    Let \(M\) be a Kripke model and \(\mathit{env}\) an environment assigning a predicate on worlds to every index. At a game position \((\varphi, w, st)\), where \(st\) is the stack of enclosing fixpoint binders and \(|st|\) its length, Verifier wins exactly when \(\varphi\) is an atom \(a\) true at \(w\), a negated atom \(\neg a\) with \(a\) false at \(w\), \(\top\), a box \(\Box\psi\), or a variable with de Bruijn index \(n \ge |st|\) such that \(\mathit{env}(n - |st|)\) holds at \(w\). At every other position (a variable with index \(n < |st|\), \(\bot\), a diamond, a conjunction, a disjunction or a fixpoint) Refuter wins.

    The condition is consulted only at positions with no moves: a box with no successors leaves Refuter stuck and a diamond with no successors leaves Verifier stuck, while a bound variable, a conjunction, a disjunction and a fixpoint always have a move. The environment index is shifted by \(|st|\) because each binder on the stack shifts de Bruijn indices by one.

    def TerminalWin (M : KripkeModel World Atom) (env : ℕ → World → Prop) :
    GamePosition Atom World → Prop
  | ⟨.atom a, w, _⟩ => M.valuation w a
  | ⟨.natom a, w, _⟩ => ¬ M.valuation w a
  | ⟨.var n, w, st⟩ => if n < st.length then False else env (n - st.length) w
  | ⟨.top, _, _⟩ => True
  | ⟨.bot, _, _⟩ => False
  | ⟨.box _, _, _⟩ => True
  | ⟨.dia _, _, _⟩ => False
  | _ => False
    structure KripkeModel (World : Type u) (Atom : Type v) where
  /-- There is at least one world. -/
  nonempty_world : Nonempty World
  /-- The accessibility relation. -/
  rel : World → World → Prop
  /-- The atoms true at each world. -/
  valuation : World → Atom → Prop

    DefinitionParity winning condition#✓fully proved

    Cslib.Modal.MuCalculus.Game.ParityWin

    For an infinite sequence of game positions \(p_0, p_1, \dots\), Verifier wins by the parity condition when there is an even priority \(e\) such that infinitely many positions \(p_n\) have priority \(e\), and every priority \(e' < e\) is the priority of only finitely many positions \(p_n\). Equivalently, some priority occurs infinitely often and the least such priority is even; in particular, if no priority occurs infinitely often, Verifier does not win. Only fixpoint-binder positions carry a priority, so the other positions do not affect the outcome.

    The least-priority convention is what makes outer binders dominate: an outer binder has a smaller priority than any binder nested inside it.

    def ParityWin (play : ℕ → GamePosition Atom World) : Prop :=
  ∃ e, Even e ∧ (∀ N, ∃ n, N ≤ n ∧ prio (play n) = some e) ∧
    ∀ e', e' < e → ∃ N, ∀ n, N ≤ n → prio (play n) ≠ some e'
    abbrev GamePosition (Atom World : Type u) :=
  MuFormula Atom × World × List (MuFormula Atom)

    StructureVerifier strategy#✓fully proved

    Cslib.Modal.MuCalculus.Game.Strategy

    A Verifier strategy for the model-checking game on a Kripke model \(M\) is a map \(\sigma\) from game positions to game positions such that, at every Verifier-owned position \(p\) from which some move exists, there is a move from \(p\) to \(\sigma(p)\).

    The move-respecting condition is what makes Verifier's winning meaningful: without it, a strategy could "win" by choosing a non-move at a Verifier position, leaving no legal play at all.

    structure Strategy (M : KripkeModel World Atom) where
  /-- The chosen successor at a Verifier-owned position. -/
  choose : GamePosition Atom World → GamePosition Atom World
  /-- The chosen successor is a legal move whenever one exists. -/
  choose_move : ∀ p, Owner p = true → (∃ q, Move M p q) → Move M p (choose p)
    structure KripkeModel (World : Type u) (Atom : Type v) where
  /-- There is at least one world. -/
  nonempty_world : Nonempty World
  /-- The accessibility relation. -/
  rel : World → World → Prop
  /-- The atoms true at each world. -/
  valuation : World → Atom → Prop

    InductiveFinite plays consistent with a strategy#✓fully proved

    Cslib.Modal.MuCalculus.Game.PlayFrom

    Fix a Kripke model \(M\) and a Verifier strategy \(\sigma\). The finite plays from a position \(p\) that follow \(\sigma\) are the lists of positions defined inductively: the one-element list \([p]\) is such a play; and if the game has a move from \(p\) to \(q\), where \(q\) is the position chosen by \(\sigma\) at \(p\) whenever Verifier owns \(p\), then \(p\) followed by any such play from \(q\) is such a play from \(p\).

    A play need not end at a position with no moves: every finite prefix counts.

    inductive PlayFrom (M : KripkeModel World Atom) (σ : Strategy M) :
    GamePosition Atom World → List (GamePosition Atom World) → Prop
  /-- The trivial play. -/
  | single {p : GamePosition Atom World} : PlayFrom M σ p [p]
  /-- A move followed by a play from the successor. -/
  | cons {p q : GamePosition Atom World} {ps : List (GamePosition Atom World)}
      (h : Move M p q) (hσ : Owner p = true → q = σ.choose p) (hps : PlayFrom M σ q ps) :
      PlayFrom M σ p (p :: ps)
    structure KripkeModel (World : Type u) (Atom : Type v) where
  /-- There is at least one world. -/
  nonempty_world : Nonempty World
  /-- The accessibility relation. -/
  rel : World → World → Prop
  /-- The atoms true at each world. -/
  valuation : World → Atom → Prop

  /-- The chosen successor at a Verifier-owned position. -/
  choose : GamePosition Atom World → GamePosition Atom World
  /-- The chosen successor is a legal move whenever one exists. -/
  choose_move : ∀ p, Owner p = true → (∃ q, Move M p q) → Move M p (choose p)

    inductive Move (M : KripkeModel World Atom) :
    GamePosition Atom World → GamePosition Atom World → Prop
  /-- Refuter chooses a conjunct. -/
  | andLeft {φ ψ : MuFormula Atom} {w : World} {st : List (MuFormula Atom)} :
      Move M ⟨.and φ ψ, w, st⟩ ⟨φ, w, st⟩
  /-- Refuter chooses a conjunct. -/
  | andRight {φ ψ : MuFormula Atom} {w : World} {st : List (MuFormula Atom)} :
      Move M ⟨.and φ ψ, w, st⟩ ⟨ψ, w, st⟩
  /-- Verifier chooses a disjunct. -/
  | orLeft {φ ψ : MuFormula Atom} {w : World} {st : List (MuFormula Atom)} :
      Move M ⟨.or φ ψ, w, st⟩ ⟨φ, w, st⟩
  /-- Verifier chooses a disjunct. -/
  ⋯

    DefinitionWinning strategy from a position#✓fully proved

    Cslib.Modal.MuCalculus.Game.StrategyWins

    Let \(M\) be a Kripke model, \(\mathit{env}\) an environment assigning a predicate on worlds to every index, \(\sigma\) a Verifier strategy for the model-checking game on \(M\) and \(p\) a game position. A play is consistent with \(\sigma\) when each step is a move and, at every Verifier-owned position, goes to the position chosen by \(\sigma\). The strategy \(\sigma\) wins from \(p\) in \(\mathit{env}\) when every finite play from \(p\) consistent with \(\sigma\) whose last position has no moves ends in a position that is a terminal win for Verifier in \(\mathit{env}\), and every infinite play from \(p\) consistent with \(\sigma\) satisfies the parity condition.

    def StrategyWins (M : KripkeModel World Atom) (env : ℕ → World → Prop)
    (σ : Strategy M) (p : GamePosition Atom World) : Prop :=
  (∀ ps : List (GamePosition Atom World), PlayFrom M σ p ps →
    (∃ q, playLast ps = some q ∧ NoMoves M q) →
      ∃ q, playLast ps = some q ∧ TerminalWin M env q) ∧
  (∀ play : ℕ → GamePosition Atom World, play 0 = p →
    (∀ n, Move M (play n) (play (n + 1)) ∧
      (Owner (play n) = true → play (n + 1) = σ.choose (play n))) →
      ParityWin play)
    structure KripkeModel (World : Type u) (Atom : Type v) where
  /-- There is at least one world. -/
  nonempty_world : Nonempty World
  /-- The accessibility relation. -/
  rel : World → World → Prop
  /-- The atoms true at each world. -/
  valuation : World → Atom → Prop

    CSLibExt/Logics/Modal/MuCalculusGame.lean:174

    def TerminalWin (M : KripkeModel World Atom) (env : ℕ → World → Prop) :
    GamePosition Atom World → Prop
  | ⟨.atom a, w, _⟩ => M.valuation w a
  | ⟨.natom a, w, _⟩ => ¬ M.valuation w a
  | ⟨.var n, w, st⟩ => if n < st.length then False else env (n - st.length) w
  | ⟨.top, _, _⟩ => True
  | ⟨.bot, _, _⟩ => False
  | ⟨.box _, _, _⟩ => True
  | ⟨.dia _, _, _⟩ => False
  | _ => False

    def ParityWin (play : ℕ → GamePosition Atom World) : Prop :=
  ∃ e, Even e ∧ (∀ N, ∃ n, N ≤ n ∧ prio (play n) = some e) ∧
    ∀ e', e' < e → ∃ N, ∀ n, N ≤ n → prio (play n) ≠ some e'

    DefinitionVerifier has a winning strategy#✓fully proved

    Cslib.Modal.MuCalculus.Game.VerifierWins

    For a Kripke model \(M\), an environment \(\mathit{env}\) and a game position \(p\), Verifier wins from \(p\) when some Verifier strategy \(\sigma\) in the model-checking game on \(M\) wins from \(p\) in environment \(\mathit{env}\): every complete finite \(\sigma\)-consistent play from \(p\) ends at a terminal winning position, and every infinite \(\sigma\)-consistent play from \(p\) satisfies the parity condition.

    def VerifierWins (M : KripkeModel World Atom) (env : ℕ → World → Prop)
    (p : GamePosition Atom World) : Prop :=
  ∃ σ : Strategy M, StrategyWins M env σ p

    Closed formulas and the regression for nesting-depth priorities

    DefinitionFormulas closed at depth \(d\)#✓fully proved

    Cslib.Modal.MuCalculus.Game.ClosedAt

    Closedness of a μ-calculus formula \(\varphi\) (with de Bruijn variables) at a depth \(d \in \mathbb{N}\) is defined by recursion on \(\varphi\): atoms, negated atoms, \(\bot\) and \(\top\) are closed at every depth; the variable with index \(n\) is closed at depth \(d\) exactly when \(n < d\); \(\varphi \wedge \psi\) and \(\varphi \vee \psi\) are closed at depth \(d\) when both \(\varphi\) and \(\psi\) are; \(\Box\varphi\) and \(\Diamond\varphi\) are closed at depth \(d\) when \(\varphi\) is; and \(\mu\varphi\) and \(\nu\varphi\) are closed at depth \(d\) when \(\varphi\) is closed at depth \(d + 1\). Thus every free variable of \(\varphi\) refers to one of \(d\) enclosing binders.

    def ClosedAt : MuFormula Atom → ℕ → Prop
  | .atom _, _ => True
  | .natom _, _ => True
  | .var n, d => n < d
  | .bot, _ => True
  | .top, _ => True
  | .and φ ψ, d => ClosedAt φ d ∧ ClosedAt ψ d
  | .or φ ψ, d => ClosedAt φ d ∧ ClosedAt ψ d
  | .box φ, d => ClosedAt φ d
  | .dia φ, d => ClosedAt φ d
  | .mu φ, d => ClosedAt φ (d + 1)
  | .nu φ, d => ClosedAt φ (d + 1)
    inductive MuFormula (Atom : Type u) : Type u where
  | atom : Atom → MuFormula Atom
  | natom : Atom → MuFormula Atom
  | var : ℕ → MuFormula Atom
  | bot : MuFormula Atom
  | top : MuFormula Atom
  | and : MuFormula Atom → MuFormula Atom → MuFormula Atom
  | or : MuFormula Atom → MuFormula Atom → MuFormula Atom
  | box : MuFormula Atom → MuFormula Atom
  | dia : MuFormula Atom → MuFormula Atom
  | mu : MuFormula Atom → MuFormula Atom
  | nu : MuFormula Atom → MuFormula Atom

    CSLibExt/Logics/Modal/MuCalculus.lean:55

    DefinitionClosed formulas#✓fully proved

    Cslib.Modal.MuCalculus.Game.Closed

    A μ-calculus formula \(\varphi\) is closed when it is closed at depth \(0\), that is, when it has no free variables.

    def Closed (φ : MuFormula Atom) : Prop := ClosedAt φ 0

    TheoremClosedness is monotone in the depth#✓fully proved

    Cslib.Modal.MuCalculus.Game.ClosedAt.mono

    Let \(\varphi\) be a μ-calculus formula and let \(d \le d'\) be natural numbers. If \(\varphi\) is closed at depth \(d\) (every free variable of \(\varphi\) is less than \(d\)), then \(\varphi\) is closed at depth \(d'\): more enclosing binders can only bind more variables.

    theorem ClosedAt.mono {φ : MuFormula Atom} {d d' : ℕ} (h : d ≤ d')
    (hc : ClosedAt φ d) : ClosedAt φ d'

    TheoremSatisfaction depends only on the environment below the closure depth#✓fully proved

    Cslib.Modal.MuCalculus.Game.sat_eq_of_closedAt

    Let \(M\) be a Kripke model, \(\varphi\) a μ-calculus formula and \(d\) a natural number such that \(\varphi\) is closed at depth \(d\): every occurrence of a variable with de Bruijn index \(n\) lying under \(k\) fixpoint binders of \(\varphi\) satisfies \(n < d + k\). If two environments \(\mathit{env}\) and \(\mathit{env}'\), each assigning a predicate on worlds to every index, agree at every index \(n < d\), then for every world \(w\), \(\varphi\) is satisfied at \(w\) in \(M\) under \(\mathit{env}\) if and only if it is satisfied at \(w\) in \(M\) under \(\mathit{env}'\).

    In particular, satisfaction of a closed formula is independent of the environment.

    theorem sat_eq_of_closedAt (M : KripkeModel World Atom) :
    ∀ (φ : MuFormula Atom) {env env' : ℕ → World → Prop} {d : ℕ},
      (∀ n, n < d → env n = env' n) → ClosedAt φ d → ∀ w,
        sat M env φ w ↔ sat M env' φ w
    structure KripkeModel (World : Type u) (Atom : Type v) where
  /-- There is at least one world. -/
  nonempty_world : Nonempty World
  /-- The accessibility relation. -/
  rel : World → World → Prop
  /-- The atoms true at each world. -/
  valuation : World → Atom → Prop

  | .atom _, _ => True
  | .natom _, _ => True
  | .var n, d => n < d

  | .or φ ψ, d => ClosedAt φ d ∧ ClosedAt ψ d
  | .box φ, d => ClosedAt φ d

  | .nu φ, d => ClosedAt φ (d + 1)

    CSLibExt/Logics/Modal/MuCalculusGame.lean:258

    def sat (env : ℕ → World → Prop) : MuFormula Atom → (World → Prop)
  | .atom a => fun w => M.valuation w a
  | .natom a => fun w => ¬ M.valuation w a
  | .var n => env n
  | .bot => fun _ => False
  | .top => fun _ => True
  | .and φ ψ => fun w => sat env φ w ∧ sat env ψ w
  | .or φ ψ => fun w => sat env φ w ∨ sat env ψ w
  | .box φ => fun w => ∀ v, M.rel w v → sat env φ v
  | .dia φ => fun w => ∃ v, M.rel w v ∧ sat env φ v
  | .mu φ => fun w => ∀ S : World → Prop,
      (∀ v, sat (extendEnv env S) φ v → S v) → S w
  ⋯

    TheoremClosed formulas are environment-independent#✓fully proved

    Cslib.Modal.MuCalculus.Game.sat_closed

    Let \(M\) be a Kripke model and \(\varphi\) a closed μ-calculus formula, that is, one with no free variables. For any two environments \(\mathrm{env}\) and \(\mathrm{env}'\), each assigning a set of worlds to every variable index, and every world \(w\), \(\varphi\) is satisfied at \(w\) in \(M\) under \(\mathrm{env}\) if and only if it is satisfied at \(w\) in \(M\) under \(\mathrm{env}'\).

    theorem sat_closed (M : KripkeModel World Atom) {φ : MuFormula Atom}
    (hφ : Closed φ) (env env' : ℕ → World → Prop) (w : World) :
    sat M env φ w ↔ sat M env' φ w

  /-- There is at least one world. -/
  nonempty_world : Nonempty World
  /-- The accessibility relation. -/

  /-- The atoms true at each world. -/

    def Closed (φ : MuFormula Atom) : Prop := ClosedAt φ 0

    CSLibExt/Logics/Modal/MuCalculusGame.lean:272

    def sat (env : ℕ → World → Prop) : MuFormula Atom → (World → Prop)
  | .atom a => fun w => M.valuation w a
  | .natom a => fun w => ¬ M.valuation w a
  | .var n => env n
  | .bot => fun _ => False
  | .top => fun _ => True
  | .and φ ψ => fun w => sat env φ w ∧ sat env ψ w
  | .or φ ψ => fun w => sat env φ w ∨ sat env ψ w
  | .box φ => fun w => ∀ v, M.rel w v → sat env φ v
  | .dia φ => fun w => ∃ v, M.rel w v ∧ sat env φ v
  | .mu φ => fun w => ∀ S : World → Prop,
      (∀ v, sat (extendEnv env S) φ v → S v) → S w
  ⋯

  /-- There is at least one world. -/

  /-- The accessibility relation. -/

  /-- The atoms true at each world. -/

    DefinitionA least fixpoint over a vacuous greatest fixpoint#✓fully proved

    Cslib.Modal.MuCalculus.Game.counterexampleFormula

    The μ-calculus formula \(\mu X.\, \nu Y.\, X\) over a one-element type of atoms: a least fixpoint whose body is a greatest fixpoint whose body is the variable \(X\) bound by the outer least fixpoint, so the variable \(Y\) bound by the inner greatest fixpoint never occurs.

    Its denotation is empty: the outer functional is the identity, and the least fixed point of the identity on predicates is the empty predicate.

    def counterexampleFormula : MuFormula Unit := .mu (.nu (.var 1))

    DefinitionStep map of the regression play#✓fully proved

    Cslib.Modal.MuCalculus.Game.ceNext

    Let \(\Phi = \mu X.\, \nu Y.\, X\) be the regression formula over the one-element type of atoms, and write game positions over the one-element type of worlds as (formula, world, stack of enclosing binders, innermost first). For every world \(w\), the step map sends \((\Phi, w, [\,])\) to \((\nu Y.\, X, w, [\Phi])\), sends \((\nu Y.\, X, w, [\Phi])\) to \((X, w, [\nu Y.\, X, \Phi])\), and sends \((X, w, [\nu Y.\, X, \Phi])\) to \((\Phi, w, [\,])\), where \(X\) is the variable bound by the outer \(\mu\); every other game position is sent to itself.

    def ceNext : GamePosition Unit Unit → GamePosition Unit Unit
  | ⟨.mu (.nu (.var 1)), w, []⟩ => ⟨.nu (.var 1), w, [.mu (.nu (.var 1))]⟩
  | ⟨.nu (.var 1), w, [.mu (.nu (.var 1))]⟩ =>
      ⟨.var 1, w, [.nu (.var 1), .mu (.nu (.var 1))]⟩
  | ⟨.var 1, w, [.nu (.var 1), .mu (.nu (.var 1))]⟩ =>
      ⟨.mu (.nu (.var 1)), w, []⟩
  | p => p
    abbrev GamePosition (Atom World : Type u) :=
  MuFormula Atom × World × List (MuFormula Atom)

    DefinitionThe regression play#✓fully proved

    Cslib.Modal.MuCalculus.Game.cePlay

    Write \(\ast\) for the unique world, and consider the game positions (formula, world, binder stack listed innermost first) \(P_0 = (\mu X.\,\nu Y.\,X,\ \ast,\ [\,])\), \(P_1 = (\nu Y.\,X,\ \ast,\ [\mu X.\,\nu Y.\,X])\) and \(P_2 = (X,\ \ast,\ [\nu Y.\,X,\ \mu X.\,\nu Y.\,X])\). Let \(s\) be the step map sending \(P_0\) to \(P_1\), \(P_1\) to \(P_2\) and \(P_2\) to \(P_0\), and fixing every other position. The regression play is the sequence of positions \(p_0 = P_0\), \(p_{n+1} = s(p_n)\), which cycles through \(P_0, P_1, P_2\).

    These are the successive positions of the model-checking game for \(\mu X.\,\nu Y.\,X\) on the one-world model.

    def cePlay : ℕ → GamePosition Unit Unit
  | 0 => cePos0
  | n + 1 => ceNext (cePlay n)
    abbrev GamePosition (Atom World : Type u) :=
  MuFormula Atom × World × List (MuFormula Atom)

    TheoremThe regression play is a play#✓fully proved

    Cslib.Modal.MuCalculus.Game.cePlay_moves

    For every \(n \in \mathbb{N}\), in the model-checking game on the one-world regression model there is a move from the position at step \(n\) of the regression play to the position at step \(n + 1\).

    theorem cePlay_moves (n : ℕ) :
    Move counterexampleModel (cePlay n) (cePlay (n + 1))
    inductive Move (M : KripkeModel World Atom) :
    GamePosition Atom World → GamePosition Atom World → Prop
  /-- Refuter chooses a conjunct. -/
  | andLeft {φ ψ : MuFormula Atom} {w : World} {st : List (MuFormula Atom)} :
      Move M ⟨.and φ ψ, w, st⟩ ⟨φ, w, st⟩
  /-- Refuter chooses a conjunct. -/
  | andRight {φ ψ : MuFormula Atom} {w : World} {st : List (MuFormula Atom)} :
      Move M ⟨.and φ ψ, w, st⟩ ⟨ψ, w, st⟩
  /-- Verifier chooses a disjunct. -/
  | orLeft {φ ψ : MuFormula Atom} {w : World} {st : List (MuFormula Atom)} :
      Move M ⟨.or φ ψ, w, st⟩ ⟨φ, w, st⟩
  /-- Verifier chooses a disjunct. -/
  ⋯

    TheoremPriority of the outer binder in the regression play#✓fully proved

    Cslib.Modal.MuCalculus.Game.ce_prio0

    The starting position of the regression play, consisting of the formula \(\mu X.\,\nu Y.\,X\), the unique world and the empty binder stack, has priority \(1\): a \(\mu\)-binder position with \(k\) binders on the stack has priority \(2k + 1\), and here \(k = 0\).

    Under fixed priorities (\(\mu \mapsto 1\), \(\nu \mapsto 2\)) the greatest priority recurring on this play would be \(2\) (even, Verifier wins); with priorities increasing with nesting depth (the inner \(\nu\) here has priority \(4\)) and the least-priority rule, the least binder priority recurring on this play is \(1\) (odd, Refuter wins).

    theorem ce_prio0 : prio (cePos0 : GamePosition Unit Unit) = some 1
    def prio : GamePosition Atom World → Option ℕ
  | ⟨.mu _, _, st⟩ => some (2 * st.length + 1)
  | ⟨.nu _, _, st⟩ => some (2 * st.length + 2)
  | _ => none

    CSLibExt/Logics/Modal/MuCalculusGame.lean:168

    def cePos0 : GamePosition Unit Unit :=
  ⟨.mu (.nu (.var 1)), Unit.unit, []⟩

    theorem ce_prio1 : prio (cePos1 : GamePosition Unit Unit) = some 4

    TheoremVerifier loses the infinite play of \(\mu X.\, \nu Y.\, X\)#✓fully proved

    Cslib.Modal.MuCalculus.Game.counterexample_parityWin_false

    Consider the infinite sequence of positions of the model-checking game for \(\mu X.\, \nu Y.\, X\) (with one-element types of worlds and atoms) that starts at this formula with empty binder stack and repeats three steps: unfold \(\mu X\), unfold \(\nu Y\), and follow the variable \(X\) back to the formula with empty stack. This play does not satisfy the parity condition: it is not the case that the least binder priority occurring infinitely often is even.

    theorem counterexample_parityWin_false : ¬ ParityWin cePlay
    def ParityWin (play : ℕ → GamePosition Atom World) : Prop :=
  ∃ e, Even e ∧ (∀ N, ∃ n, N ≤ n ∧ prio (play n) = some e) ∧
    ∀ e', e' < e → ∃ N, ∀ n, N ≤ n → prio (play n) ≠ some e'

    Theorem\(\mu X.\, \nu Y.\, X\) is false in the one-world model#✓fully proved

    Cslib.Modal.MuCalculus.Game.counterexample_sat_false

    In the Kripke model with a single world, which is related to itself, and a single atom, which is false there, under the environment that assigns the empty set to every variable, the formula \(\mu X.\, \nu Y.\, X\) is not satisfied at the unique world.

    This agrees with the model-checking game, where Verifier loses the play of this formula.

    theorem counterexample_sat_false :
    ¬ sat counterexampleModel (fun _ => fun _ => False)
      counterexampleFormula Unit.unit
    def sat (env : ℕ → World → Prop) : MuFormula Atom → (World → Prop)
  | .atom a => fun w => M.valuation w a
  | .natom a => fun w => ¬ M.valuation w a
  | .var n => env n
  | .bot => fun _ => False
  | .top => fun _ => True
  | .and φ ψ => fun w => sat env φ w ∧ sat env ψ w
  | .or φ ψ => fun w => sat env φ w ∨ sat env ψ w
  | .box φ => fun w => ∀ v, M.rel w v → sat env φ v
  | .dia φ => fun w => ∃ v, M.rel w v ∧ sat env φ v
  | .mu φ => fun w => ∀ S : World → Prop,
      (∀ v, sat (extendEnv env S) φ v → S v) → S w
  ⋯

    def counterexampleModel : KripkeModel Unit Unit where
  nonempty_world := ⟨Unit.unit⟩
  rel := fun _ _ => True
  valuation := fun _ _ => False

    The fixpoint-free fragment

    InductiveFixpoint-free formulas#✓fully proved

    Cslib.Modal.MuCalculus.Game.FixFreeFormula

    The fixpoint-free formulas over a type of atoms are built from atoms \(p\), negated atoms \(\neg p\), \(\bot\) and \(\top\) by conjunction \(\varphi \land \psi\), disjunction \(\varphi \lor \psi\), necessity \(\Box\varphi\) and possibility \(\Diamond\varphi\).

    These are the formulas of basic modal logic in negation normal form: the μ-calculus formulas with no fixpoint operators and no variables.

    Cslib.Modal.MuCalculus.Game.FixFreeFormula · 17 lines 
    inductive FixFreeFormula (Atom : Type u) : Type u where
  /-- An atom. -/
  | atom : Atom → FixFreeFormula Atom

  /-- Falsity. -/

  /-- Truth. -/

  /-- Disjunction. -/
  | or : FixFreeFormula Atom → FixFreeFormula Atom → FixFreeFormula Atom
  /-- Necessity. -/
  | box : FixFreeFormula Atom → FixFreeFormula Atom
  /-- Possibility. -/
  | dia : FixFreeFormula Atom → FixFreeFormula Atom

    DefinitionEmbedding of the fixpoint-free fragment into the μ-calculus#✓fully proved

    Cslib.Modal.MuCalculus.Game.FixFreeFormula.toMu

    The map from fixpoint-free formulas to μ-calculus formulas defined by recursion: atoms, negated atoms, \(\bot\) and \(\top\) go to the same atom, negated atom, \(\bot\) and \(\top\), and \(\varphi \wedge \psi\), \(\varphi \vee \psi\), \(\Box \varphi\) and \(\Diamond \varphi\) go to the conjunction, disjunction, box and diamond of the images of \(\varphi\) and \(\psi\).

    def toMu : FixFreeFormula Atom → MuFormula Atom
  | .atom a => .atom a
  | .natom a => .natom a
  | .bot => .bot
  | .top => .top
  | .and φ ψ => .and (toMu φ) (toMu ψ)
  | .or φ ψ => .or (toMu φ) (toMu ψ)
  | .box φ => .box (toMu φ)
  | .dia φ => .dia (toMu φ)

  /-- A negated atom. -/
  | natom : Atom → FixFreeFormula Atom
  /-- Falsity. -/

  /-- Truth. -/
  | top : FixFreeFormula Atom
  /-- Conjunction. -/
  | and : FixFreeFormula Atom → FixFreeFormula Atom → FixFreeFormula Atom
  /-- Disjunction. -/

    DefinitionBackward-induction winning region of the fixpoint-free fragment#✓fully proved

    Cslib.Modal.MuCalculus.Game.Wins

    Let \(M\) be a Kripke model with accessibility relation \(R\). For a fixpoint-free formula \(\varphi\) and a world \(w\), "\(\varphi\) is winning at \(w\)" is defined by recursion on \(\varphi\): an atom \(a\) is winning at \(w\) iff \(a\) is true at \(w\) in \(M\); a negated atom \(\lnot a\) iff \(a\) is not true at \(w\); \(\bot\) is never winning and \(\top\) is always winning; \(\varphi \land \psi\) is winning at \(w\) iff both \(\varphi\) and \(\psi\) are; \(\varphi \lor \psi\) iff \(\varphi\) or \(\psi\) is; \(\Box \varphi\) iff \(\varphi\) is winning at every \(v\) with \(w \mathrel{R} v\); and \(\Diamond \varphi\) iff \(\varphi\) is winning at some \(v\) with \(w \mathrel{R} v\).

    Verifier has a winning strategy in the model-checking game from the position \((\varphi, w, [])\) with empty stack exactly when \(\varphi\) is winning at \(w\).

    def Wins (M : KripkeModel World Atom) : FixFreeFormula Atom → World → Prop
  | .atom a, w => M.valuation w a
  | .natom a, w => ¬ M.valuation w a
  | .bot, _ => False
  | .top, _ => True
  | .and φ ψ, w => Wins M φ w ∧ Wins M ψ w
  | .or φ ψ, w => Wins M φ w ∨ Wins M ψ w
  | .box φ, w => ∀ v, M.rel w v → Wins M φ v
  | .dia φ, w => ∃ v, M.rel w v ∧ Wins M φ v
    structure KripkeModel (World : Type u) (Atom : Type v) where
  /-- There is at least one world. -/
  nonempty_world : Nonempty World
  /-- The accessibility relation. -/
  rel : World → World → Prop
  /-- The atoms true at each world. -/
  valuation : World → Atom → Prop

    CSLibExt/Logics/Modal/Kripke.lean:83

    TheoremBackward-induction winning equals satisfaction on the fixpoint-free fragment#✓fully proved

    Cslib.Modal.MuCalculus.Game.wins_iff_sat

    For every Kripke model \(M\), environment \(\mathit{env}\), fixpoint-free formula \(\varphi\) and world \(w\), \(w\) lies in the backward-induction winning region of \(\varphi\) if and only if \(w\) satisfies the μ-calculus embedding of \(\varphi\) in \(M\) under \(\mathit{env}\).

    theorem wins_iff_sat (M : KripkeModel World Atom) (env : ℕ → World → Prop)
    (φ : FixFreeFormula Atom) (w : World) :
    Wins M φ w ↔ sat M env (FixFreeFormula.toMu φ) w
    structure KripkeModel (World : Type u) (Atom : Type v) where
  /-- There is at least one world. -/
  nonempty_world : Nonempty World
  /-- The accessibility relation. -/
  rel : World → World → Prop
  /-- The atoms true at each world. -/
  valuation : World → Atom → Prop

    The Wins-guided strategy

    DefinitionSize of a μ-calculus formula#✓fully proved

    Cslib.Modal.MuCalculus.Game.MuFormula.size

    The size \(|\varphi|\) of a μ-calculus formula \(\varphi\) is the number of nodes of its syntax tree: an atom, a negated atom, a variable, \(\bot\) and \(\top\) have size \(1\); \(|\varphi \wedge \psi| = |\varphi \vee \psi| = |\varphi| + |\psi| + 1\); and \(|\Box\varphi| = |\Diamond\varphi| = |\mu\varphi| = |\nu\varphi| = |\varphi| + 1\).

    def MuFormula.size : MuFormula Atom → ℕ
  | .atom _ | .natom _ | .var _ | .bot | .top => 1
  | .and φ ψ | .or φ ψ => MuFormula.size φ + MuFormula.size ψ + 1
  | .box φ | .dia φ | .mu φ | .nu φ => MuFormula.size φ + 1
    inductive MuFormula (Atom : Type u) : Type u where
  | atom : Atom → MuFormula Atom
  | natom : Atom → MuFormula Atom
  | var : ℕ → MuFormula Atom
  | bot : MuFormula Atom
  | top : MuFormula Atom
  | and : MuFormula Atom → MuFormula Atom → MuFormula Atom
  | or : MuFormula Atom → MuFormula Atom → MuFormula Atom
  | box : MuFormula Atom → MuFormula Atom
  | dia : MuFormula Atom → MuFormula Atom
  | mu : MuFormula Atom → MuFormula Atom
  | nu : MuFormula Atom → MuFormula Atom

    DefinitionPartial inverse of the fixpoint-free embedding#✓fully proved

    Cslib.Modal.MuCalculus.Game.toMuInv

    The partial map from μ-calculus formulas to fixpoint-free formulas defined by recursion: an atom, a negated atom, \(\bot\) and \(\top\) are sent to the same formula; a conjunction or disjunction is sent to the conjunction or disjunction of the values at its two parts, and \(\Box\psi\), \(\Diamond\psi\) to the box or diamond of the value at \(\psi\), whenever those values are defined; the map is undefined at variables and at least and greatest fixpoints, and at every formula with a part where it is undefined.

    It inverts the embedding of fixpoint-free formulas into μ-calculus formulas on its image.

    def toMuInv : MuFormula Atom → Option (FixFreeFormula Atom)
  | .atom a => some (.atom a)
  | .natom a => some (.natom a)
  | .bot => some .bot
  | .top => some .top
  | .and φ ψ => (toMuInv φ).bind fun φ' => (toMuInv ψ).map (FixFreeFormula.and φ')
  | .or φ ψ => (toMuInv φ).bind fun φ' => (toMuInv ψ).map (FixFreeFormula.or φ')
  | .box φ => (toMuInv φ).map FixFreeFormula.box
  | .dia φ => (toMuInv φ).map FixFreeFormula.dia
  | _ => none
    inductive MuFormula (Atom : Type u) : Type u where
  | atom : Atom → MuFormula Atom
  | natom : Atom → MuFormula Atom
  | var : ℕ → MuFormula Atom
  | bot : MuFormula Atom
  | top : MuFormula Atom
  | and : MuFormula Atom → MuFormula Atom → MuFormula Atom
  | or : MuFormula Atom → MuFormula Atom → MuFormula Atom
  | box : MuFormula Atom → MuFormula Atom
  | dia : MuFormula Atom → MuFormula Atom
  | mu : MuFormula Atom → MuFormula Atom
  | nu : MuFormula Atom → MuFormula Atom

    TheoremThe embedding inverts the partial inverse#✓fully proved

    Cslib.Modal.MuCalculus.Game.toMu_toMuInv

    TheoremThe fixpoint-free embedding is injective#✓fully proved

    Cslib.Modal.MuCalculus.Game.FixFreeFormula.toMu_injective

    The embedding of fixpoint-free formulas into μ-calculus formulas is injective: distinct fixpoint-free formulas have distinct images.

    This is what lets the canonical fragment game recover a fragment formula from an embedded game position.

    theorem FixFreeFormula.toMu_injective :
    Function.Injective (FixFreeFormula.toMu (Atom := Atom))
    def toMu : FixFreeFormula Atom → MuFormula Atom
  | .atom a => .atom a
  | .natom a => .natom a
  | .bot => .bot
  | .top => .top
  | .and φ ψ => .and (toMu φ) (toMu ψ)
  | .or φ ψ => .or (toMu φ) (toMu ψ)
  | .box φ => .box (toMu φ)
  | .dia φ => .dia (toMu φ)

    DefinitionWins-guided move#✓fully proved

    Cslib.Modal.MuCalculus.Game.winsMove

    Let \(M\) be a Kripke model, and identify each fixpoint-free formula with its embedding into the μ-calculus. For a μ-calculus formula \(\psi\), a world \(w\) and a stack \(s\) of fixpoint binders, the Wins-guided move at the game position \((\psi, w, s)\) is the following position. If \(s\) is empty and \(\psi\) is a fixpoint-free disjunction \(\psi_1 \lor \psi_2\), it is \((\psi_1, w, [])\) when \(\psi_1\) is winning at \(w\) (in the backward-induction winning region of the fixpoint-free fragment) and \((\psi_2, w, [])\) otherwise. If \(s\) is empty and \(\psi\) is a fixpoint-free formula \(\Diamond \psi_1\), it is \((\psi_1, v, [])\) for a chosen successor \(v\) of \(w\) at which \(\psi_1\) is winning, if one exists; otherwise \((\psi_1, v, [])\) for a chosen successor \(v\) of \(w\), if one exists; otherwise \((\psi, w, [])\). In every other case: at a disjunction \(\varphi_1 \lor \varphi_2\) it is \((\varphi_1, w, s)\); at \(\Diamond \varphi_1\) it is \((\varphi_1, v, s)\) for a chosen successor \(v\) of \(w\) if one exists, and \((\psi, w, s)\) otherwise; at \(\nu \varphi_1\) it is \((\varphi_1, w, s')\), where \(s'\) is \(s\) with \(\nu \varphi_1\) pushed on top; and at every remaining formula (the positions owned by Refuter) it is \((\psi, w, s)\).

    Only the left disjunct is tested: if a fixpoint-free disjunction is winning at \(w\) and its left disjunct is not, then its right disjunct is.

    Cslib.Modal.MuCalculus.Game.winsMove · 29 lines 
    noncomputable def winsMove (M : KripkeModel World Atom) (ψ : MuFormula Atom) (w : World)
    (st : List (MuFormula Atom)) : GamePosition Atom World :=
  match ψ, st with
  | .or φ₁ φ₂, [] =>
      match toMuInv (.or φ₁ φ₂) with
      | some (.or ψ₁ ψ₂) =>
          if _ : Wins M ψ₁ w then
            (⟨FixFreeFormula.toMu ψ₁, w, []⟩ : GamePosition Atom World)
          else (⟨FixFreeFormula.toMu ψ₂, w, []⟩ : GamePosition Atom World)
      | _ => (⟨φ₁, w, []⟩ : GamePosition Atom World)
  | .dia φ₁, [] =>
      match toMuInv (.dia φ₁) with
      | some (.dia ψ₁) =>
          if h : ∃ v, M.rel w v ∧ Wins M ψ₁ v then
            (⟨FixFreeFormula.toMu ψ₁, Classical.choose h, []⟩ : GamePosition Atom World)
          else if hsucc : ∃ v, M.rel w v then
            (⟨φ₁, Classical.choose hsucc, []⟩ : GamePosition Atom World)
          else (⟨.dia φ₁, w, []⟩ : GamePosition Atom World)
      | _ =>
          if h : ∃ v, M.rel w v then
            (⟨φ₁, Classical.choose h, []⟩ : GamePosition Atom World)
          else (⟨.dia φ₁, w, []⟩ : GamePosition Atom World)
  | .or φ₁ _, st => (⟨φ₁, w, st⟩ : GamePosition Atom World)
  | .dia φ₁, st =>
      if h : ∃ v, M.rel w v then
        (⟨φ₁, Classical.choose h, st⟩ : GamePosition Atom World)
      else (⟨.dia φ₁, w, st⟩ : GamePosition Atom World)
  | .nu φ₁, st => (⟨φ₁, w, .nu φ₁ :: st⟩ : GamePosition Atom World)
  | _, _ => (⟨ψ, w, st⟩ : GamePosition Atom World)
    structure KripkeModel (World : Type u) (Atom : Type v) where
  /-- There is at least one world. -/
  nonempty_world : Nonempty World
  /-- The accessibility relation. -/
  rel : World → World → Prop
  /-- The atoms true at each world. -/
  valuation : World → Atom → Prop

    CSLibExt/Logics/Modal/Kripke.lean:83

    inductive MuFormula (Atom : Type u) : Type u where
  | atom : Atom → MuFormula Atom
  | natom : Atom → MuFormula Atom
  | var : ℕ → MuFormula Atom
  | bot : MuFormula Atom
  | top : MuFormula Atom
  | and : MuFormula Atom → MuFormula Atom → MuFormula Atom
  | or : MuFormula Atom → MuFormula Atom → MuFormula Atom
  | box : MuFormula Atom → MuFormula Atom
  | dia : MuFormula Atom → MuFormula Atom
  | mu : MuFormula Atom → MuFormula Atom
  | nu : MuFormula Atom → MuFormula Atom

    abbrev GamePosition (Atom World : Type u) :=
  MuFormula Atom × World × List (MuFormula Atom)

    CSLibExt/Logics/Modal/MuCalculusGame.lean:108

    def toMuInv : MuFormula Atom → Option (FixFreeFormula Atom)
  | .atom a => some (.atom a)
  | .natom a => some (.natom a)
  | .bot => some .bot
  | .top => some .top
  | .and φ ψ => (toMuInv φ).bind fun φ' => (toMuInv ψ).map (FixFreeFormula.and φ')
  | .or φ ψ => (toMuInv φ).bind fun φ' => (toMuInv ψ).map (FixFreeFormula.or φ')
  | .box φ => (toMuInv φ).map FixFreeFormula.box
  | .dia φ => (toMuInv φ).map FixFreeFormula.dia
  | _ => none

    CSLibExt/Logics/Modal/MuCalculusGame.lean:593

    def Wins (M : KripkeModel World Atom) : FixFreeFormula Atom → World → Prop
  | .atom a, w => M.valuation w a
  | .natom a, w => ¬ M.valuation w a
  | .bot, _ => False
  | .top, _ => True
  | .and φ ψ, w => Wins M φ w ∧ Wins M ψ w
  | .or φ ψ, w => Wins M φ w ∨ Wins M ψ w
  | .box φ, w => ∀ v, M.rel w v → Wins M φ v
  | .dia φ, w => ∃ v, M.rel w v ∧ Wins M φ v

    CSLibExt/Logics/Modal/MuCalculusGame.lean:550

    def toMu : FixFreeFormula Atom → MuFormula Atom
  | .atom a => .atom a
  | .natom a => .natom a
  | .bot => .bot
  | .top => .top
  | .and φ ψ => .and (toMu φ) (toMu ψ)
  | .or φ ψ => .or (toMu φ) (toMu ψ)
  | .box φ => .box (toMu φ)
  | .dia φ => .dia (toMu φ)

    TheoremThe Wins-guided move at a disjunction#✓fully proved

    Cslib.Modal.MuCalculus.Game.winsMove_or

    Let \(M\) be a Kripke model, \(\varphi_1\) and \(\varphi_2\) fixpoint-free formulas and \(w\) a world in the backward-induction winning region of \(\varphi_1 \vee \varphi_2\). Then for some \(i \in \{1, 2\}\), \(w\) lies in the backward-induction winning region of \(\varphi_i\) and the Wins-guided move at the position made of the embedding of \(\varphi_1 \vee \varphi_2\), the world \(w\) and the empty binder stack is the position made of the embedding of \(\varphi_i\), the world \(w\) and the empty stack.

    theorem winsMove_or {M : KripkeModel World Atom} {φ₁ φ₂ : FixFreeFormula Atom} {w : World}
    (hW : Wins M (.or φ₁ φ₂) w) :
    (winsMove M (FixFreeFormula.toMu (.or φ₁ φ₂)) w [] =
        ⟨FixFreeFormula.toMu φ₁, w, []⟩ ∧ Wins M φ₁ w) ∨
    (winsMove M (FixFreeFormula.toMu (.or φ₁ φ₂)) w [] =
        ⟨FixFreeFormula.toMu φ₂, w, []⟩ ∧ Wins M φ₂ w)
    structure KripkeModel (World : Type u) (Atom : Type v) where
  /-- There is at least one world. -/
  nonempty_world : Nonempty World
  /-- The accessibility relation. -/
  rel : World → World → Prop
  /-- The atoms true at each world. -/
  valuation : World → Atom → Prop

    Cslib.Modal.MuCalculus.Game.winsMove_dia

    Let \(M\) be a Kripke model, \(\varphi\) a fixpoint-free formula and \(w\) a world in the backward-induction winning region of \(\Diamond \varphi\). Then there is a successor \(v\) of \(w\) in \(M\) lying in the backward-induction winning region of \(\varphi\) such that the Wins-guided move at the position made of the embedding of \(\Diamond \varphi\), the world \(w\) and the empty binder stack is the position made of the embedding of \(\varphi\), the world \(v\) and the empty stack.

    theorem winsMove_dia {M : KripkeModel World Atom} {φ₁ : FixFreeFormula Atom} {w : World}
    (hW : Wins M (.dia φ₁) w) :
    ∃ v, winsMove M (FixFreeFormula.toMu (.dia φ₁)) w [] =
        ⟨FixFreeFormula.toMu φ₁, v, []⟩ ∧ M.rel w v ∧ Wins M φ₁ v

    Cslib.Modal.MuCalculus.Game.winsMove_isMove

    For a Kripke model \(M\) and a game position \(p\) owned by Verifier from which some move exists, the Wins-guided move at \(p\) is a legal move of the model-checking game on \(M\) from \(p\).

    theorem winsMove_isMove (M : KripkeModel World Atom) (p : GamePosition Atom World)
    (hOwn : Owner p = true) (hExists : ∃ q, Move M p q) :
    Move M p (winsMove M p.1 p.2.1 p.2.2)

    CSLibExt/Logics/Modal/MuCalculusGame.lean:149

    inductive Move (M : KripkeModel World Atom) :
    GamePosition Atom World → GamePosition Atom World → Prop
  /-- Refuter chooses a conjunct. -/
  | andLeft {φ ψ : MuFormula Atom} {w : World} {st : List (MuFormula Atom)} :
      Move M ⟨.and φ ψ, w, st⟩ ⟨φ, w, st⟩
  /-- Refuter chooses a conjunct. -/
  | andRight {φ ψ : MuFormula Atom} {w : World} {st : List (MuFormula Atom)} :
      Move M ⟨.and φ ψ, w, st⟩ ⟨ψ, w, st⟩
  /-- Verifier chooses a disjunct. -/
  | orLeft {φ ψ : MuFormula Atom} {w : World} {st : List (MuFormula Atom)} :
      Move M ⟨.or φ ψ, w, st⟩ ⟨φ, w, st⟩
  /-- Verifier chooses a disjunct. -/
  ⋯

    DefinitionWins-guided strategy#✓fully proved

    Cslib.Modal.MuCalculus.Game.winsStrategy

    For a Kripke model \(M\), the Wins-guided strategy is the Verifier strategy of the model-checking game on \(M\) that chooses, at each game position \((\psi, w, s)\), the Wins-guided move at \((\psi, w, s)\). At every Verifier-owned position that has a move, this choice is a legal move.

    noncomputable def winsStrategy (M : KripkeModel World Atom) : Strategy M where
  choose p := winsMove M p.1 p.2.1 p.2.2
  choose_move p hOwn hExists := winsMove_isMove M p hOwn hExists
    structure KripkeModel (World : Type u) (Atom : Type v) where
  /-- There is at least one world. -/
  nonempty_world : Nonempty World
  /-- The accessibility relation. -/
  rel : World → World → Prop
  /-- The atoms true at each world. -/
  valuation : World → Atom → Prop

    CSLibExt/Logics/Modal/Kripke.lean:83

    structure Strategy (M : KripkeModel World Atom) where
  /-- The chosen successor at a Verifier-owned position. -/
  choose : GamePosition Atom World → GamePosition Atom World
  /-- The chosen successor is a legal move whenever one exists. -/
  choose_move : ∀ p, Owner p = true → (∃ q, Move M p q) → Move M p (choose p)

    TheoremFinite plays are nonempty#✓fully proved

    Cslib.Modal.MuCalculus.Game.PlayFrom.ne_nil

    Let \(M\) be a Kripke model, \(\sigma\) a Verifier strategy and \(p\) a game position. Every finite play from \(p\) consistent with \(\sigma\) is a nonempty list of positions.

    theorem PlayFrom.ne_nil {M : KripkeModel World Atom} {σ : Strategy M}
    {p : GamePosition Atom World} {ps : List (GamePosition Atom World)}
    (h : PlayFrom M σ p ps) : ps ≠ []

    CSLibExt/Logics/Modal/MuCalculusGame.lean:108

    inductive PlayFrom (M : KripkeModel World Atom) (σ : Strategy M) :
    GamePosition Atom World → List (GamePosition Atom World) → Prop
  /-- The trivial play. -/
  | single {p : GamePosition Atom World} : PlayFrom M σ p [p]
  /-- A move followed by a play from the successor. -/
  | cons {p q : GamePosition Atom World} {ps : List (GamePosition Atom World)}
      (h : Move M p q) (hσ : Owner p = true → q = σ.choose p) (hps : PlayFrom M σ q ps) :
      PlayFrom M σ p (p :: ps)

    Cslib.Modal.MuCalculus.Game.move_of_toMu

    Let \(M\) be a Kripke model, \(\varphi\) a fixpoint-free formula and \(w\) a world. Every move of the model-checking game in \(M\) from the position \((\varphi, w, [\,])\), with \(\varphi\) embedded in the μ-calculus and the empty stack, leads to a position \((\varphi', w', [\,])\) for some fixpoint-free formula \(\varphi'\) and world \(w'\), and the size of \(\varphi'\) is strictly smaller than the size of \(\varphi\) (sizes of the embedded μ-calculus formulas).

    theorem move_of_toMu {M : KripkeModel World Atom} {φ : FixFreeFormula Atom} {w : World}
    {q : GamePosition Atom World} (h : Move M ⟨FixFreeFormula.toMu φ, w, []⟩ q) :
    ∃ φ' : FixFreeFormula Atom, ∃ w' : World, q = ⟨FixFreeFormula.toMu φ', w', []⟩ ∧
      MuFormula.size (FixFreeFormula.toMu φ') < MuFormula.size (FixFreeFormula.toMu φ)
    structure KripkeModel (World : Type u) (Atom : Type v) where
  /-- There is at least one world. -/
  nonempty_world : Nonempty World
  /-- The accessibility relation. -/
  rel : World → World → Prop
  /-- The atoms true at each world. -/
  valuation : World → Atom → Prop

    CSLibExt/Logics/Modal/Kripke.lean:83

    inductive FixFreeFormula (Atom : Type u) : Type u where
  /-- An atom. -/
  | atom : Atom → FixFreeFormula Atom
  /-- A negated atom. -/
  | natom : Atom → FixFreeFormula Atom
  /-- Falsity. -/
  | bot : FixFreeFormula Atom
  /-- Truth. -/
  | top : FixFreeFormula Atom
  /-- Conjunction. -/
  | and : FixFreeFormula Atom → FixFreeFormula Atom → FixFreeFormula Atom
  /-- Disjunction. -/
  ⋯

    CSLibExt/Logics/Modal/MuCalculusGame.lean:108

    inductive Move (M : KripkeModel World Atom) :
    GamePosition Atom World → GamePosition Atom World → Prop
  /-- Refuter chooses a conjunct. -/
  | andLeft {φ ψ : MuFormula Atom} {w : World} {st : List (MuFormula Atom)} :
      Move M ⟨.and φ ψ, w, st⟩ ⟨φ, w, st⟩
  /-- Refuter chooses a conjunct. -/
  | andRight {φ ψ : MuFormula Atom} {w : World} {st : List (MuFormula Atom)} :
      Move M ⟨.and φ ψ, w, st⟩ ⟨ψ, w, st⟩
  /-- Verifier chooses a disjunct. -/
  | orLeft {φ ψ : MuFormula Atom} {w : World} {st : List (MuFormula Atom)} :
      Move M ⟨.or φ ψ, w, st⟩ ⟨φ, w, st⟩
  /-- Verifier chooses a disjunct. -/
  ⋯

    def toMu : FixFreeFormula Atom → MuFormula Atom
  | .atom a => .atom a
  | .natom a => .natom a
  | .bot => .bot
  | .top => .top
  | .and φ ψ => .and (toMu φ) (toMu ψ)
  | .or φ ψ => .or (toMu φ) (toMu ψ)
  | .box φ => .box (toMu φ)
  | .dia φ => .dia (toMu φ)

    CSLibExt/Logics/Modal/MuCalculusGame.lean:536

    def MuFormula.size : MuFormula Atom → ℕ
  | .atom _ | .natom _ | .var _ | .bot | .top => 1
  | .and φ ψ | .or φ ψ => MuFormula.size φ + MuFormula.size ψ + 1
  | .box φ | .dia φ | .mu φ | .nu φ => MuFormula.size φ + 1

    Cslib.Modal.MuCalculus.Game.no_infinite_play_of_fixFree

    Let \(M\) be a Kripke model, \(\varphi\) a fixpoint-free formula and \(w\) a world. There is no sequence of game positions \(p_0, p_1, p_2, \dots\) with \(p_0 = (\varphi, w, [\,])\) (the embedded formula with the empty stack) such that each step from \(p_n\) to \(p_{n+1}\) is a move of the model-checking game in \(M\). The reason is that every move strictly decreases the formula size.

    theorem no_infinite_play_of_fixFree {M : KripkeModel World Atom} {φ : FixFreeFormula Atom}
    {w : World} {play : ℕ → GamePosition Atom World}
    (h0 : play 0 = ⟨FixFreeFormula.toMu φ, w, []⟩)
    (hsteps : ∀ n, Move M (play n) (play (n + 1))) : False

  /-- The atoms true at each world. -/
  valuation : World → Atom → Prop

    Cslib.Modal.MuCalculus.Game.strategyWins_of_move

    Let \(M\) be a Kripke model, \(\mathit{env}\) an environment and \(\sigma\) a Verifier strategy for the model-checking game on \(M\). If \(\sigma\) wins from a position \(p\) in \(\mathit{env}\), and \(q\) is a position reachable from \(p\) by one move that is the position chosen by \(\sigma\) at \(p\) whenever Verifier owns \(p\), then \(\sigma\) also wins from \(q\) in \(\mathit{env}\).

    theorem strategyWins_of_move {M : KripkeModel World Atom} {env : ℕ → World → Prop}
    {σ : Strategy M} {p q : GamePosition Atom World} (hW : StrategyWins M env σ p)
    (hmove : Move M p q) (hσ : Owner p = true → q = σ.choose p) :
    StrategyWins M env σ q

  /-- The atoms true at each world. -/

    structure Strategy (M : KripkeModel World Atom) where
  /-- The chosen successor at a Verifier-owned position. -/
  choose : GamePosition Atom World → GamePosition Atom World
  /-- The chosen successor is a legal move whenever one exists. -/
  choose_move : ∀ p, Owner p = true → (∃ q, Move M p q) → Move M p (choose p)

    CSLibExt/Logics/Modal/MuCalculusGame.lean:108

    def StrategyWins (M : KripkeModel World Atom) (env : ℕ → World → Prop)
    (σ : Strategy M) (p : GamePosition Atom World) : Prop :=
  (∀ ps : List (GamePosition Atom World), PlayFrom M σ p ps →
    (∃ q, playLast ps = some q ∧ NoMoves M q) →
      ∃ q, playLast ps = some q ∧ TerminalWin M env q) ∧
  (∀ play : ℕ → GamePosition Atom World, play 0 = p →
    (∀ n, Move M (play n) (play (n + 1)) ∧
      (Owner (play n) = true → play (n + 1) = σ.choose (play n))) →
      ParityWin play)

    def Wins (M : KripkeModel World Atom) : FixFreeFormula Atom → World → Prop
  | .atom a, w => M.valuation w a
  | .natom a, w => ¬ M.valuation w a
  | .bot, _ => False
  | .top, _ => True
  | .and φ ψ, w => Wins M φ w ∧ Wins M ψ w
  | .or φ ψ, w => Wins M φ w ∨ Wins M ψ w
  | .box φ, w => ∀ v, M.rel w v → Wins M φ v
  | .dia φ, w => ∃ v, M.rel w v ∧ Wins M φ v

    CSLibExt/Logics/Modal/MuCalculusGame.lean:174

    def TerminalWin (M : KripkeModel World Atom) (env : ℕ → World → Prop) :
    GamePosition Atom World → Prop
  | ⟨.atom a, w, _⟩ => M.valuation w a
  | ⟨.natom a, w, _⟩ => ¬ M.valuation w a
  | ⟨.var n, w, st⟩ => if n < st.length then False else env (n - st.length) w
  | ⟨.top, _, _⟩ => True
  | ⟨.bot, _, _⟩ => False
  | ⟨.box _, _, _⟩ => True
  | ⟨.dia _, _, _⟩ => False
  | _ => False

    Cslib.Modal.MuCalculus.Game.wins_implies_verifierWins

    Let \(M\) be a Kripke model, \(\mathit{env}\) an environment assigning a set of worlds to each variable index, \(\varphi\) a fixpoint-free formula and \(w\) a world in the backward-induction winning region of \(\varphi\). Then Verifier has a winning strategy, in environment \(\mathit{env}\), in the model-checking game on \(M\) from the position made of the μ-calculus embedding of \(\varphi\), the world \(w\) and the empty binder stack.

    theorem wins_implies_verifierWins (M : KripkeModel World Atom)
    (env : ℕ → World → Prop) (φ : FixFreeFormula Atom)
    (w : World) (hW : Wins M φ w) :
    VerifierWins M env ⟨FixFreeFormula.toMu φ, w, []⟩
    structure KripkeModel (World : Type u) (Atom : Type v) where
  /-- There is at least one world. -/
  nonempty_world : Nonempty World
  /-- The accessibility relation. -/
  rel : World → World → Prop
  /-- The atoms true at each world. -/
  valuation : World → Atom → Prop

    inductive FixFreeFormula (Atom : Type u) : Type u where
  /-- An atom. -/
  | atom : Atom → FixFreeFormula Atom
  /-- A negated atom. -/
  | natom : Atom → FixFreeFormula Atom
  /-- Falsity. -/
  | bot : FixFreeFormula Atom
  /-- Truth. -/
  | top : FixFreeFormula Atom
  /-- Conjunction. -/
  | and : FixFreeFormula Atom → FixFreeFormula Atom → FixFreeFormula Atom
  /-- Disjunction. -/
  ⋯

    CSLibExt/Logics/Modal/MuCalculusGame.lean:515

    def Wins (M : KripkeModel World Atom) : FixFreeFormula Atom → World → Prop
  | .atom a, w => M.valuation w a
  | .natom a, w => ¬ M.valuation w a
  | .bot, _ => False
  | .top, _ => True
  | .and φ ψ, w => Wins M φ w ∧ Wins M ψ w
  | .or φ ψ, w => Wins M φ w ∨ Wins M ψ w
  | .box φ, w => ∀ v, M.rel w v → Wins M φ v
  | .dia φ, w => ∃ v, M.rel w v ∧ Wins M φ v

    CSLibExt/Logics/Modal/MuCalculusGame.lean:550

    def VerifierWins (M : KripkeModel World Atom) (env : ℕ → World → Prop)
    (p : GamePosition Atom World) : Prop :=
  ∃ σ : Strategy M, StrategyWins M env σ p

    def toMu : FixFreeFormula Atom → MuFormula Atom
  | .atom a => .atom a
  | .natom a => .natom a
  | .bot => .bot
  | .top => .top
  | .and φ ψ => .and (toMu φ) (toMu ψ)
  | .or φ ψ => .or (toMu φ) (toMu ψ)
  | .box φ => .box (toMu φ)
  | .dia φ => .dia (toMu φ)

    TheoremInversion for disjunction moves#✓fully proved

    Cslib.Modal.MuCalculus.Game.Move.or_cases

    Let \(M\) be a Kripke model, \(\varphi\) and \(\psi\) μ-calculus formulas, \(w\) a world and \(s\) a stack of fixpoint binders. If going from the game position \((\varphi \vee \psi, w, s)\) to a position \(q\) is a move of the model-checking game in \(M\), then \(q\) is \((\varphi, w, s)\) or \((\psi, w, s)\): the world and the stack are unchanged.

    theorem Move.or_cases {M : KripkeModel World Atom} {φ ψ : MuFormula Atom} {w : World}
    {st : List (MuFormula Atom)} {q : GamePosition Atom World}
    (h : Move M ⟨.or φ ψ, w, st⟩ q) : q = ⟨φ, w, st⟩ ∨ q = ⟨ψ, w, st⟩

    inductive Move (M : KripkeModel World Atom) :
    GamePosition Atom World → GamePosition Atom World → Prop
  /-- Refuter chooses a conjunct. -/
  | andLeft {φ ψ : MuFormula Atom} {w : World} {st : List (MuFormula Atom)} :
      Move M ⟨.and φ ψ, w, st⟩ ⟨φ, w, st⟩
  /-- Refuter chooses a conjunct. -/
  | andRight {φ ψ : MuFormula Atom} {w : World} {st : List (MuFormula Atom)} :
      Move M ⟨.and φ ψ, w, st⟩ ⟨ψ, w, st⟩
  /-- Verifier chooses a disjunct. -/
  | orLeft {φ ψ : MuFormula Atom} {w : World} {st : List (MuFormula Atom)} :
      Move M ⟨.or φ ψ, w, st⟩ ⟨φ, w, st⟩
  /-- Verifier chooses a disjunct. -/

    TheoremMoves from a diamond position#✓fully proved

    Cslib.Modal.MuCalculus.Game.Move.dia_cases

    In the model-checking game on a Kripke model \(M\), every move from the position with formula \(\Diamond\varphi\), world \(w\) and binder stack \(s\) goes to the position with formula \(\varphi\), world \(v\) and the same stack \(s\), for some world \(v\) accessible from \(w\) in \(M\).

    theorem Move.dia_cases {M : KripkeModel World Atom} {φ : MuFormula Atom} {w : World}
    {st : List (MuFormula Atom)} {q : GamePosition Atom World}
    (h : Move M ⟨.dia φ, w, st⟩ q) :
    ∃ v, M.rel w v ∧ q = ⟨φ, v, st⟩

    TheoremGame-vs-satisfaction, converse direction#✓fully proved

    Cslib.Modal.MuCalculus.Game.verifierWins_implies_wins

    Let \(M\) be a Kripke model, \(\mathit{env}\) an environment assigning a set of worlds to each variable index, \(\varphi\) a fixpoint-free formula and \(w\) a world.     TheoremBackward-induction winning equals game winning on the fixpoint-free fragment#✓fully proved

    Cslib.Modal.MuCalculus.Game.wins_iff_verifierWins

    For every Kripke model \(M\), environment \(\mathit{env}\), fixpoint-free formula \(\varphi\) and world \(w\), \(w\) lies in the backward-induction winning region of \(\varphi\) if and only if Verifier has a winning strategy, in environment \(\mathit{env}\), in the model-checking game on \(M\) from the position made of the μ-calculus embedding of \(\varphi\), the world \(w\) and the empty binder stack.

    theorem wins_iff_verifierWins (M : KripkeModel World Atom)

    The fragment game as a canonical parity game

    InductivePositions of the totalized fragment game#✓fully proved

    Cslib.Modal.MuCalculus.Game.FragPos

    A position of the totalized fragment game, over a type of atoms and a type of worlds, is either an embedded position \((\varphi, w)\) of a fixpoint-free formula \(\varphi\) and a world \(w\), standing for the model-checking game position \((\varphi, w, [])\) with \(\varphi\) embedded as a μ-calculus formula and the empty fixpoint stack, or one of two absorbing states: the winning state, where Verifier has won, and the losing state, where Refuter has won.

    Since the arena contains no fixpoint positions and every move between embedded positions strictly decreases the formula, priority \(0\) on embedded positions is sound. An embedded position with no moves leads to the winning state if it is a terminal win for Verifier and to the losing state otherwise, and both absorbing states loop to themselves, so the game has no dead ends and is a parity game.

    inductive FragPos (Atom World : Type u) where
  /-- An embedded fragment position `(φ, w)`, standing for `⟨toMu φ, w, []⟩`. -/
  | pos : FixFreeFormula Atom → World → FragPos Atom World
  /-- Absorbing Verifier-wins state. -/
  | win : FragPos Atom World
  /-- Absorbing Refuter-wins state. -/
  | lose : FragPos Atom World
    inductive FixFreeFormula (Atom : Type u) : Type u where
  /-- An atom. -/
  | atom : Atom → FixFreeFormula Atom
  /-- A negated atom. -/
  | natom : Atom → FixFreeFormula Atom
  /-- Falsity. -/
  | bot : FixFreeFormula Atom
  /-- Truth. -/
  | top : FixFreeFormula Atom
  /-- Conjunction. -/
  | and : FixFreeFormula Atom → FixFreeFormula Atom → FixFreeFormula Atom
  /-- Disjunction. -/
  ⋯

    CSLibExt/Logics/Modal/MuCalculusGame.lean:515

    DefinitionProjection of game positions onto the fragment arena#✓fully proved

    Cslib.Modal.MuCalculus.Game.fragOfGamePos

    A game position with empty binder stack whose formula is the μ-calculus reading of a fixpoint-free formula \(\varphi\), at world \(w\), is sent to the embedded position \((\varphi, w)\). Every other game position (one whose formula contains a fixpoint operator or a variable, or one with a nonempty stack) is sent to the losing state.

    The losing state is a default that is never reached on plays that stay in the arena, since every move from an embedded position lands on an embedded position.

    def fragOfGamePos : GamePosition Atom World → FragPos Atom World
  | ⟨ψ, w, []⟩ =>
      match toMuInv ψ with
      | some φ => .pos φ w
      | none => .lose
  | ⟨_, _, _ :: _⟩ => .lose
    abbrev GamePosition (Atom World : Type u) :=
  MuFormula Atom × World × List (MuFormula Atom)

    CSLibExt/Logics/Modal/MuCalculusGame.lean:108

    inductive FragPos (Atom World : Type u) where
  /-- An embedded fragment position `(φ, w)`, standing for `⟨toMu φ, w, []⟩`. -/
  | pos : FixFreeFormula Atom → World → FragPos Atom World
  /-- Absorbing Verifier-wins state. -/
  | win : FragPos Atom World
  /-- Absorbing Refuter-wins state. -/
  | lose : FragPos Atom World

    CSLibExt/Logics/Modal/MuCalculusGame.lean:1205

    def toMuInv : MuFormula Atom → Option (FixFreeFormula Atom)
  | .atom a => some (.atom a)
  | .natom a => some (.natom a)
  | .bot => some .bot
  | .top => some .top
  | .and φ ψ => (toMuInv φ).bind fun φ' => (toMuInv ψ).map (FixFreeFormula.and φ')
  | .or φ ψ => (toMuInv φ).bind fun φ' => (toMuInv ψ).map (FixFreeFormula.or φ')
  | .box φ => (toMuInv φ).map FixFreeFormula.box
  | .dia φ => (toMuInv φ).map FixFreeFormula.dia
  | _ => none

    InductiveMoves of the totalized fragment game#✓fully proved

    Cslib.Modal.MuCalculus.Game.FragMove

    Let \(M\) be a Kripke model and \(e\) an environment, and read each fixpoint-free formula \(\varphi\) as a μ-calculus formula. The totalized fragment game of \(M\) and \(e\) has these moves: from an embedded position \((\varphi, w)\) to \((\varphi', w')\) whenever the model-checking game of \(M\) has a move from \((\varphi, w)\) to \((\varphi', w')\) with empty binder stacks; from \((\varphi, w)\) to the winning state when the game position \((\varphi, w)\) with empty stack has no moves and is won by Verifier in environment \(e\); from \((\varphi, w)\) to the losing state when that game position has no moves and is not won by Verifier; and from each of the winning and losing states to itself.

    Cslib.Modal.MuCalculus.Game.FragMove · 21 lines 
    inductive FragMove (M : KripkeModel World Atom) (env : ℕ → World → Prop) :
    FragPos Atom World → FragPos Atom World → Prop
  /-- A game move between embedded fragment positions. -/
  | pos_move {φ φ' : FixFreeFormula Atom} {w w' : World}
      (h : Move M ⟨FixFreeFormula.toMu φ, w, []⟩
        ⟨FixFreeFormula.toMu φ', w', []⟩) :
      FragMove M env (.pos φ w) (.pos φ' w')
  /-- A terminal winning position moves to the absorbing win state. -/
  | toWin {φ : FixFreeFormula Atom} {w : World}
      (hNo : NoMoves M ⟨FixFreeFormula.toMu φ, w, []⟩)
      (hWin : TerminalWin M env ⟨FixFreeFormula.toMu φ, w, []⟩) :
      FragMove M env (.pos φ w) .win
  /-- A terminal losing position moves to the absorbing lose state. -/
  | toLose {φ : FixFreeFormula Atom} {w : World}
      (hNo : NoMoves M ⟨FixFreeFormula.toMu φ, w, []⟩)
      (hLose : ¬ TerminalWin M env ⟨FixFreeFormula.toMu φ, w, []⟩) :
      FragMove M env (.pos φ w) .lose
  /-- The win state loops. -/
  | win_self : FragMove M env .win .win
  /-- The lose state loops. -/
  | lose_self : FragMove M env .lose .lose
    structure KripkeModel (World : Type u) (Atom : Type v) where
  /-- There is at least one world. -/
  nonempty_world : Nonempty World
  /-- The accessibility relation. -/

  /-- The atoms true at each world. -/

  | bot : FixFreeFormula Atom
  /-- Truth. -/

  /-- Conjunction. -/

    CSLibExt/Logics/Modal/MuCalculusGame.lean:515

    inductive Move (M : KripkeModel World Atom) :
    GamePosition Atom World → GamePosition Atom World → Prop
  /-- Refuter chooses a conjunct. -/
  | andLeft {φ ψ : MuFormula Atom} {w : World} {st : List (MuFormula Atom)} :
      Move M ⟨.and φ ψ, w, st⟩ ⟨φ, w, st⟩
  /-- Refuter chooses a conjunct. -/
  | andRight {φ ψ : MuFormula Atom} {w : World} {st : List (MuFormula Atom)} :
      Move M ⟨.and φ ψ, w, st⟩ ⟨ψ, w, st⟩
  /-- Verifier chooses a disjunct. -/
  | orLeft {φ ψ : MuFormula Atom} {w : World} {st : List (MuFormula Atom)} :
      Move M ⟨.or φ ψ, w, st⟩ ⟨φ, w, st⟩
  /-- Verifier chooses a disjunct. -/
  ⋯

    CSLibExt/Logics/Modal/MuCalculusGame.lean:116

    def toMu : FixFreeFormula Atom → MuFormula Atom
  | .atom a => .atom a
  | .natom a => .natom a
  | .bot => .bot
  | .top => .top
  | .and φ ψ => .and (toMu φ) (toMu ψ)
  | .or φ ψ => .or (toMu φ) (toMu ψ)
  | .box φ => .box (toMu φ)
  | .dia φ => .dia (toMu φ)

    CSLibExt/Logics/Modal/MuCalculusGame.lean:536

    def NoMoves (M : KripkeModel World Atom) (p : GamePosition Atom World) : Prop :=
  ∀ q, ¬ Move M p q

    CSLibExt/Logics/Modal/MuCalculusGame.lean:174

    def TerminalWin (M : KripkeModel World Atom) (env : ℕ → World → Prop) :
    GamePosition Atom World → Prop
  | ⟨.atom a, w, _⟩ => M.valuation w a
  | ⟨.natom a, w, _⟩ => ¬ M.valuation w a
  | ⟨.var n, w, st⟩ => if n < st.length then False else env (n - st.length) w
  | ⟨.top, _, _⟩ => True
  | ⟨.bot, _, _⟩ => False
  | ⟨.box _, _, _⟩ => True
  | ⟨.dia _, _, _⟩ => False
  | _ => False

    DefinitionOwners in the totalized fragment game#✓fully proved

    Cslib.Modal.MuCalculus.Game.fragOwner

    In the totalized fragment game, an embedded position \((\varphi, w)\) of a fixpoint-free formula \(\varphi\) at a world \(w\) is owned by the even player if Verifier owns the model-checking game position \((\varphi, w, [])\) (with \(\varphi\) embedded as a μ-calculus formula and the empty fixpoint stack), and by the odd player otherwise, that is, when Refuter owns it. The absorbing winning and losing states are both owned by the even player.

    Verifier plays the even player; the moves at the absorbing states are forced, so their owner does not matter.

    def fragOwner : FragPos Atom World → Cslib.Games.Player
  | .pos φ w =>
      if Owner ⟨FixFreeFormula.toMu φ, w, []⟩ = true then
        Cslib.Games.Player.even
      else Cslib.Games.Player.odd
  | .win => Cslib.Games.Player.even
  | .lose => Cslib.Games.Player.even
    inductive FragPos (Atom World : Type u) where
  /-- An embedded fragment position `(φ, w)`, standing for `⟨toMu φ, w, []⟩`. -/
  | pos : FixFreeFormula Atom → World → FragPos Atom World
  /-- Absorbing Verifier-wins state. -/
  | win : FragPos Atom World
  /-- Absorbing Refuter-wins state. -/
  | lose : FragPos Atom World

    CSLibExt/Logics/Modal/MuCalculusGame.lean:1205

    inductive Player where
  /-- The player who wins plays whose maximal recurring priority is even. -/
  | even
  /-- The player who wins plays whose maximal recurring priority is odd. -/
  | odd
  deriving DecidableEq, Repr

  | _ => false

    DefinitionPriorities in the totalized fragment game#✓fully proved

    Cslib.Modal.MuCalculus.Game.fragPriority

    In the totalized fragment game, every embedded position \((\varphi, w)\) of a fixpoint-free formula \(\varphi\) at a world \(w\) has priority \(0\), the absorbing winning state has priority \(0\), and the absorbing losing state has priority \(1\).

    So an infinite play that eventually stays at the winning state is won by the even player, and one that eventually stays at the losing state by the odd player. Priority \(0\) on embedded positions is sound because the arena contains no fixpoint positions and every move between embedded positions strictly decreases the formula, so no infinite play visits only embedded positions.

    def fragPriority : FragPos Atom World → ℕ
  | .pos _ _ => 0
  | .win => 0
  | .lose => 1
    inductive FragPos (Atom World : Type u) where
  /-- An embedded fragment position `(φ, w)`, standing for `⟨toMu φ, w, []⟩`. -/
  | pos : FixFreeFormula Atom → World → FragPos Atom World
  /-- Absorbing Verifier-wins state. -/
  | win : FragPos Atom World
  /-- Absorbing Refuter-wins state. -/
  | lose : FragPos Atom World

    DefinitionFragment parity game#✓fully proved

    Cslib.Modal.MuCalculus.Game.fragmentGame

    Let \(M\) be a Kripke model and \(\rho\) an environment, and write \((\varphi, w, [])\) for the game position with formula \(\varphi\), world \(w\) and empty binder stack. The fragment game of \(M\) and \(\rho\) is the parity game on the totalized fragment positions defined as follows. An embedded position \((\varphi, w)\) moves to \((\varphi', w')\) when \((\varphi, w, [])\) has a move to \((\varphi', w', [])\) in the model-checking game on \(M\); if \((\varphi, w, [])\) has no moves, \((\varphi, w)\) moves to the winning state when \((\varphi, w, [])\) is a terminal win in \(\rho\) and to the losing state otherwise; the winning and losing states move only to themselves. The even player owns both absorbing states and the embedded positions \((\varphi, w)\) such that Verifier owns \((\varphi, w, [])\); the odd player owns the other embedded positions. Embedded positions and the winning state have priority \(0\); the losing state has priority \(1\).

    Cslib.Modal.MuCalculus.Game.fragmentGame · 22 lines 
    noncomputable def fragmentGame (M : KripkeModel World Atom)
    (env : ℕ → World → Prop) : Cslib.Games.ParityGame (FragPos Atom World) where
  owner := fragOwner
  Move := FragMove M env
  priority := fragPriority
  serial := by
    intro v
    cases v with
    | win => exact ⟨.win, FragMove.win_self⟩
    | lose => exact ⟨.lose, FragMove.lose_self⟩
    | pos φ w =>
        by_cases hExists : ∃ φ' w', Move M
            ⟨FixFreeFormula.toMu φ, w, []⟩ ⟨FixFreeFormula.toMu φ', w', []⟩
        · obtain ⟨φ', w', hq⟩ := hExists
          exact ⟨.pos φ' w', FragMove.pos_move hq⟩
        · have hNo : NoMoves M ⟨FixFreeFormula.toMu φ, w, []⟩ := by
            intro q hq
            obtain ⟨φ', w', hq', -⟩ := move_of_toMu hq
            exact hExists ⟨φ', w', by rwa [hq'] at hq⟩
          by_cases hWin : TerminalWin M env ⟨FixFreeFormula.toMu φ, w, []⟩
          · exact ⟨.win, FragMove.toWin hNo hWin⟩
          · exact ⟨.lose, FragMove.toLose hNo hWin⟩
    structure KripkeModel (World : Type u) (Atom : Type v) where
  /-- There is at least one world. -/
  nonempty_world : Nonempty World
  /-- The accessibility relation. -/
  rel : World → World → Prop
  /-- The atoms true at each world. -/
  valuation : World → Atom → Prop

    CSLibExt/Logics/Modal/Kripke.lean:83

    structure ParityGame (Pos : Type*) where
  /-- The player choosing the move at each position. -/
  owner : Pos → Player
  /-- The moves: `Move v w` iff the owner of `v` may move from `v` to `w`. -/
  Move : Pos → Pos → Prop
  /-- The priority of each position. -/
  priority : Pos → ℕ
  /-- Every position has a move. -/
  serial : ∀ v, ∃ w, Move v w

    TheoremFragment moves between embedded positions are game moves#✓fully proved

    Cslib.Modal.MuCalculus.Game.FragMove.pos_move_iff

    Let \(M\) be a Kripke model, \(\mathrm{env}\) an environment, \(\varphi, \varphi'\) fixpoint-free formulas and \(w, w'\) worlds. If the totalized fragment game has a move from the embedded position \((\varphi, w)\) to the embedded position \((\varphi', w')\), then the model-checking game has a move from \((\varphi, w, [])\) to \((\varphi', w', [])\), where the fixpoint-free formulas are viewed as μ-calculus formulas and \([]\) is the empty binder stack.

    theorem FragMove.pos_move_iff {M : KripkeModel World Atom}
    {env : ℕ → World → Prop} {φ φ' : FixFreeFormula Atom} {w w' : World}
    (h : FragMove M env (.pos φ w) (.pos φ' w')) :
    Move M ⟨FixFreeFormula.toMu φ, w, []⟩
      ⟨FixFreeFormula.toMu φ', w', []⟩

  /-- The atoms true at each world. -/
  valuation : World → Atom → Prop

      FragMove M env (.pos φ w) .win
  ⋯

    inductive Move (M : KripkeModel World Atom) :
    GamePosition Atom World → GamePosition Atom World → Prop
  /-- Refuter chooses a conjunct. -/
  | andLeft {φ ψ : MuFormula Atom} {w : World} {st : List (MuFormula Atom)} :
      Move M ⟨.and φ ψ, w, st⟩ ⟨φ, w, st⟩
  /-- Refuter chooses a conjunct. -/
  | andRight {φ ψ : MuFormula Atom} {w : World} {st : List (MuFormula Atom)} :
      Move M ⟨.and φ ψ, w, st⟩ ⟨ψ, w, st⟩
  /-- Verifier chooses a disjunct. -/
  | orLeft {φ ψ : MuFormula Atom} {w : World} {st : List (MuFormula Atom)} :
      Move M ⟨.or φ ψ, w, st⟩ ⟨φ, w, st⟩
  /-- Verifier chooses a disjunct. -/
  ⋯

    Cslib.Modal.MuCalculus.Game.FragMove.pos_toWin

    Let \(M\) be a Kripke model, \(\mathrm{env}\) an environment, \(\varphi\) a fixpoint-free formula and \(w\) a world. If the totalized fragment game has a move from the embedded position \((\varphi, w)\) to the absorbing winning state, then the game position \((\varphi, w, [])\), with \(\varphi\) viewed as a μ-calculus formula and an empty binder stack, has no moves, and Verifier wins there as a terminal position in the environment \(\mathrm{env}\).

    theorem FragMove.pos_toWin {M : KripkeModel World Atom} {env : ℕ → World → Prop}
    {φ : FixFreeFormula Atom} {w : World}
    (h : FragMove M env (.pos φ w) .win) :
    NoMoves M ⟨FixFreeFormula.toMu φ, w, []⟩ ∧
      TerminalWin M env ⟨FixFreeFormula.toMu φ, w, []⟩

  /-- The accessibility relation. -/
  rel : World → World → Prop
  /-- The atoms true at each world. -/

    inductive FragMove (M : KripkeModel World Atom) (env : ℕ → World → Prop) :
    FragPos Atom World → FragPos Atom World → Prop
  /-- A game move between embedded fragment positions. -/
  | pos_move {φ φ' : FixFreeFormula Atom} {w w' : World}
      (h : Move M ⟨FixFreeFormula.toMu φ, w, []⟩
        ⟨FixFreeFormula.toMu φ', w', []⟩) :
      FragMove M env (.pos φ w) (.pos φ' w')
  /-- A terminal winning position moves to the absorbing win state. -/
  | toWin {φ : FixFreeFormula Atom} {w : World}
      (hNo : NoMoves M ⟨FixFreeFormula.toMu φ, w, []⟩)
      (hWin : TerminalWin M env ⟨FixFreeFormula.toMu φ, w, []⟩) :
      FragMove M env (.pos φ w) .win

    CSLibExt/Logics/Modal/MuCalculusGame.lean:536

    def TerminalWin (M : KripkeModel World Atom) (env : ℕ → World → Prop) :
    GamePosition Atom World → Prop
  | ⟨.atom a, w, _⟩ => M.valuation w a
  | ⟨.natom a, w, _⟩ => ¬ M.valuation w a
  | ⟨.var n, w, st⟩ => if n < st.length then False else env (n - st.length) w
  | ⟨.top, _, _⟩ => True
  | ⟨.bot, _, _⟩ => False
  | ⟨.box _, _, _⟩ => True
  | ⟨.dia _, _, _⟩ => False
  | _ => False

    Cslib.Modal.MuCalculus.Game.fragOwner_pos_eq_even

    For every fixpoint-free formula \(\varphi\) and world \(w\), the even player owns the embedded position \((\varphi, w)\) of the totalized fragment game if and only if Verifier owns the model-checking game position \((\varphi, w, [])\), with \(\varphi\) embedded as a μ-calculus formula and the empty fixpoint stack.

    theorem fragOwner_pos_eq_even {φ : FixFreeFormula Atom} {w : World} :
    fragOwner (FragPos.pos φ w : FragPos Atom World) = Cslib.Games.Player.even ↔
      Owner ⟨FixFreeFormula.toMu φ, w, []⟩ = true

  | .pos φ w =>
      if Owner ⟨FixFreeFormula.toMu φ, w, []⟩ = true then
        Cslib.Games.Player.even
      else Cslib.Games.Player.odd
  | .win => Cslib.Games.Player.even
  | .lose => Cslib.Games.Player.even

    DefinitionCanonical next move induced by a bespoke strategy#✓fully proved

    Cslib.Modal.MuCalculus.Game.canonicalNext

    Let \(M\) be a Kripke model, \(\mathit{env}\) an environment and \(\sigma\) a Verifier strategy for the model-checking game on \(M\). Write \(\langle \psi, v \rangle\) for the game position with the embedding of a fixpoint-free formula \(\psi\), the world \(v\) and the empty binder stack. The canonical next move is the map on positions of the fragment arena defined as follows. The absorbing states \(\mathrm{win}\) and \(\mathrm{lose}\) are sent to themselves. A fragment position \((\varphi, w)\) such that \(\langle \varphi, w \rangle\) has a move to some \(\langle \varphi', w' \rangle\) is sent, if Verifier owns \(\langle \varphi, w \rangle\), to the projection into the fragment arena of the position that \(\sigma\) chooses there (an embedded position \(\langle \varphi', w' \rangle\) projects to \((\varphi', w')\), every other game position to \(\mathrm{lose}\)), and otherwise to a fragment position \((\varphi', w')\) chosen, by the axiom of choice, among those with a move from \(\langle \varphi, w \rangle\) to \(\langle \varphi', w' \rangle\). A fragment position \((\varphi, w)\) with no such move is sent to \(\mathrm{win}\) if \(\langle \varphi, w \rangle\) satisfies the terminal winning condition for Verifier in environment \(\mathit{env}\), and to \(\mathrm{lose}\) otherwise.

    noncomputable def canonicalNext (M : KripkeModel World Atom)
    (env : ℕ → World → Prop) (σ : Strategy M) : FragPos Atom World → FragPos Atom World
  | .win => .win
  | .lose => .lose
  | .pos φ w =>
      if hExists : ∃ φ' w', Move M ⟨FixFreeFormula.toMu φ, w, []⟩
          ⟨FixFreeFormula.toMu φ', w', []⟩ then
        if Owner ⟨FixFreeFormula.toMu φ, w, []⟩ = true then
          fragOfGamePos (σ.choose ⟨FixFreeFormula.toMu φ, w, []⟩)
        else
          .pos (Classical.choose hExists)
            (Classical.choose (Classical.choose_spec hExists))
      else if TerminalWin M env ⟨FixFreeFormula.toMu φ, w, []⟩ then .win else .lose
    structure KripkeModel (World : Type u) (Atom : Type v) where
  /-- There is at least one world. -/
  nonempty_world : Nonempty World
  /-- The accessibility relation. -/
  rel : World → World → Prop
  /-- The atoms true at each world. -/
  valuation : World → Atom → Prop

    CSLibExt/Logics/Modal/Kripke.lean:83

    structure Strategy (M : KripkeModel World Atom) where
  /-- The chosen successor at a Verifier-owned position. -/
  choose : GamePosition Atom World → GamePosition Atom World
  /-- The chosen successor is a legal move whenever one exists. -/
  choose_move : ∀ p, Owner p = true → (∃ q, Move M p q) → Move M p (choose p)
